EIA, Weekly Petroleum Status Report: Wednesdays at 10:30 Eastern; the week ending the Friday before. Read from the page's holiday table, 13 alternate dates.
EIA, Weekly Natural Gas Storage Report: Thursdays at 10:30 Eastern; the week ending the Friday before. Read from the page's holiday table, 9 alternate dates.
CFTC, Commitments of Traders: Fridays at 15:30 Eastern; positions as of the Tuesday before. Read from the standing rule alone: the CFTC's page lists no dates in its text. A federal holiday in the week can move the release.
The latest less the value one step before: a week for a weekly series, a month for a monthly one
The value 52 weeks before for a weekly series (364 days, the same weekday, or the nearest within 3 days), as EIA's storage report sets a week beside the same week a year before; the same month a year before for a monthly series. The price board's Year takes the newest value at least 365 days before, so for a weekly series its base can be a week older: the date of each base is on hover in both
The period's change less the five-year average change of the same period
The last 52 values held: 52 weeks for a weekly series, 52 months (over four years) for a monthly one. A week or month not held is left out and the line closes over it, so a line can span more than 52 steps; each point's date is on hover
Source: EIA, Weekly Natural Gas Storage Report (eia_gas_storage_weekly). Open its seasonal chart.

The latest less the value one step before: a week for a weekly series, a month for a monthly one
The value 52 weeks before for a weekly series (364 days, the same weekday, or the nearest within 3 days), as EIA's storage report sets a week beside the same week a year before; the same month a year before for a monthly series. The price board's Year takes the newest value at least 365 days before, so for a weekly series its base can be a week older: the date of each base is on hover in both
The period's change less the five-year average change of the same period
The last 52 values held: 52 weeks for a weekly series, 52 months (over four years) for a monthly one. A week or month not held is left out and the line closes over it, so a line can span more than 52 steps; each point's date is on hover
Source: EIA, U.S. natural gas imports and exports by point of entry and exit (eia_gas_trade_monthly); the month's volume over its days. Open its seasonal chart.

The latest less the value one step before: a week for a weekly series, a month for a monthly one
The value 52 weeks before for a weekly series (364 days, the same weekday, or the nearest within 3 days), as EIA's storage report sets a week beside the same week a year before; the same month a year before for a monthly series. The price board's Year takes the newest value at least 365 days before, so for a weekly series its base can be a week older: the date of each base is on hover in both
The period's change less the five-year average change of the same period
The last 52 values held: 52 weeks for a weekly series, 52 months (over four years) for a monthly one. A week or month not held is left out and the line closes over it, so a line can span more than 52 steps; each point's date is on hover

ICE publishes its own report for ICE Brent, and its terms of use forbid copying or republishing its site's content without written permission. Source that would supply it: ICE, under license. The Brent row above is the far smaller NYMEX contract the CFTC reports.
A region's net imports are EIA's interchange with each neighbour with its sign turned: above zero the region imported, below zero it exported. Months are EIA's Eastern months. A region's figure is EIA's sum of its balancing authorities, so it is not the figure of one of the seven grids above even where they cover nearly the same ground. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, interchange as each side filed it.
Both ends of a line between two regions file the same flow with EIA, each from its own meters and schedules, and the two filings need not agree. The section shows both and the gap between them. A gap of 10 percent or more of the mean size of the two reports is marked as large. Default: 10 percent, this page's own choice (session 8430); no publisher's threshold stands behind it.
Southeast, Florida, Carolinas, Tennessee, Northwest, Southwest: none of the seven grids of the tables above is in them, so no page drew them before 19 November 2026.
Each figure is the file's own or a sum of the file's months, MWh shown as GWh (1,000 MWh). A month the file does not hold is left out of a sum and counted beside it, never filled or scaled. Each pair's own flow and each region's own months are shown; no region is summed against another.
Region A is California and Region B is Carolinas: the first two of EIA's thirteen regions in EIA's own order of codes. The year is 2025: the last calendar year that had ended when the files were built, the newest they can hold whole (the newest year held when they hold no such year). Defaults: this page's own choices (session 8430); no publisher's rule stands behind them. The charts run over every month held whatever the year; the year sets the tables.
California, EIA's region CAL of Form EIA-930. CAISO is among its balancing authorities.
California's net imports from the neighbour as California's side filed them: EIA's interchange with its sign turned, so above zero California imported and below zero it exported. A year is the sum of the months the file holds. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, interchange as each side filed it.
The same flow as the neighbour's side filed it, with the same sign. Both ends of a line file with EIA, each from its own meters and schedules; the two need not agree.
The own report less the neighbour's, over the hours both reported.
The gap as a percent of the mean size of the two reports (the mean of their absolute values), over the months that hold both. A gap of 10 percent or more of the mean size of the two reports is marked as large. Default: 10 percent, this page's own choice (session 8430); no publisher's threshold stands behind it.
The months of the year for which the file holds the own report, of the year's months in the file. A month that is not held is left out of the sums, never filled.

Over the year the net flow between the two balancing authorities ran from this one to the other, by the file's own direction (the sign of the mean of the two reports). A balancing authority is the operator that keeps supply and demand in balance over its own area hour by hour (a utility, a federal power agency or a grid operator); each one of the lower 48 files Form EIA-930.
The one the net flow ran to over the year, by the same direction.
The flow over the year as the sending side filed it with EIA. A figure below zero says that side counted the year's net flow the other way. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, interchange as each side filed it.
The same flow as the receiving side filed it. Both ends of a line file with EIA, each from its own meters and schedules; the two need not agree.
The file's own figure for the pair: the mean of the two reports over the hours both sides reported, and the one side's report for a month in which the other reported no hour. Its sign gives the direction.
The sender's report less the receiver's over the hours both reported, as the file gives it.
The gap as a percent of the mean size of the two reports (the mean of their absolute values). A gap of 10 percent or more of the mean size of the two reports is marked as large. Default: 10 percent, this page's own choice (session 8430); no publisher's threshold stands behind it.
The months of the year that hold a report of this flow from either side, of the year's months in the file: six in 2018, whose months begin in July, and fewer in the year still running. A pair's year is the sum of EIA's Eastern months of that year, as in the net imports above. A month that is not held is left out of the sums, never filled.

The tables above this section draw no generation mix of their own: they link each of the seven grids' Mix view on the Grid page. For a region, each source's share of net generation by month stands here, from the region's own file: a chart over every month held and a table of the chosen year. The year's row is each source's energy over the year's held months as a percent of the net generation of those same months. The file holds an hour when its sources add up to its net generation within 5 percent, so a month's shares need not add up to exactly 100. A month of the mix is the region's own local month; a month of the net imports above is EIA's Eastern month.

Solar: its share of the region's net generation in the month, as the file writes it. Hover a figure for the energy. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, generation by energy source.
Hydro: its share of the region's net generation in the month, as the file writes it. Hydro includes pumped storage. Hover a figure for the energy. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, generation by energy source.
Storage: its share of the region's net generation in the month, as the file writes it. Storage is below zero in a month it charged more than it discharged. Form EIA-930 carries storage from 1 July 2024 (UTC): a month before reads not held yet, and June 2024, a local month, holds only its last hours. A region can read exactly zero for months after that: zero is what its balancing authorities filed, not a count of the batteries on its ground. Hover a figure for the energy. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, generation by energy source.
Other: its share of the region's net generation in the month, as the file writes it. Other is petroleum, geothermal, other and unknown sources. Hover a figure for the energy. Source: EIA Form EIA-930, generation by energy source.

Method: supply and trade , regions pages , interchange pairs . Decisions on this page (each with its default and its source on hover) Every series is set against three values, each actually held: the period before, the same time last year and the five-year average; nothing is filled or estimated, and a comparison not held reads "not held". (session 134) i Default: The week or month before, last year, and the five-year average. Source: docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "The three comparisons". A weekly year is 52 weeks, so a comparison is always the same weekday: last year is the value 364 days back or the nearest within 3 days, and over five years the date drifts five or six days from the calendar's. (session 134) i Default: 52 weeks back, or the nearest value within 3 days. Source: docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "The three comparisons"; EIA's own report weeks follow the same idea. The five-year average is the mean of the values 52, 104, 156, 208 and 260 weeks back (the same month of the five years before), given only when all five are held, with their lowest and highest beside it. (session 134) i Default: All five years held, or no average. Source: docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "The three comparisons". The surprise is the period's change less the mean of the same change in the five years before, marked outside when beyond their lowest or highest; the page never says "against expectations", since no analysts' consensus is held. (session 134) i Default: Against the five-year average change, never a consensus. Source: docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "The surprise": the analysts' polls are licensed. Tighter or looser is written only where the direction has a reading: more in storage, more production or more imports is looser, more exports or more fuel burned is tighter; refinery runs, the reserve and positioning carry none. (session 134) i Default: A reading only for the series whose direction has one. Source: docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "The three comparisons". Stocks are shown in million barrels and natural gas trade in Bcf a day (the month's volume over its days), so months of different length compare; commercial crude excludes the Strategic Petroleum Reserve. (session 134) i Default: Million barrels; Bcf a day; commercial crude without the reserve. Source: EIA's Weekly Petroleum Status Report and Natural Gas Monthly, converted as docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "Notes on the series", says. An LNG terminal is listed by name when it shipped at least 5,000 MMcf in a month of the last twelve; every other point of exit is one row, the total less the terminals named. (session 134) i Default: 5,000 MMcf in a month of the last twelve. Source: EIA's natural gas exports by point of exit; docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "Notes on the series". Fuel burned for power is derived, not reported: each grid's hourly gas, coal and oil generation, summed over whole local days, times a stated fleet heat rate (natural gas 7.6 MMBtu per MWh), so a grid's real fleet differs. (session 134) i Default: Stated fleet heat rates; whole local days only. Source: EIA-930's hourly generation by fuel; EIA's Electric Power Annual, Table 8.1, for the heat rates. Positioning is the CFTC's managed money, futures only, and its net; Brent is the NYMEX contract the CFTC reports, not ICE's, whose terms forbid copying its own report. (session 134) i Default: Managed money's net, futures only; NYMEX Brent. Source: CFTC, Disaggregated Commitments of Traders; ICE Futures Europe's terms of use. Day-ahead energy cleared is each operator's own figure, as a week's mean of seven whole local days ending Friday, a day counting only when every hour of it is held; the operators define it differently and are not summed. (session 136) i Default: Seven whole local days ending Friday; never summed across operators. Source: ERCOT, CAISO, NYISO, ISO-NE and SPP day-ahead market reports, as docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md, "Day-ahead energy cleared", lists them. Weekly natural gas production is not shown: EIA publishes none of its own, and the weekly supply figures in its Natural Gas Weekly Update are S&P Global's, so the row reads "licensed source needed". (session 134) i Default: The row greyed, licensed source needed. Source: EIA's Natural Gas Weekly Update credits S&P Global for its weekly supply figures. Download the tables: every public table is on Data and methods .
